### [ACCEPT] accessibility-differs-from-usability
Accessibility is distinct from usability: usability measures effectiveness and efficiency for specified users, while accessibility specifically removes barriers for people with disabilities.
- Source: entries/2026/06/20/wiki-Accessibility.md
- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accessibility

### [ACCEPT] accessibility-differs-from-universal-design
Accessibility differs from universal design: universal design creates single solutions for the widest population, while accessible design specifically eliminates barriers for disabled users.
- Source: entries/2026/06/20/wiki-Accessibility.md
- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accessibility

### [ACCEPT] assistive-vs-adaptive-technology

- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accessibility

### [ACCEPT] gibson-affordance-environment-centered-norman-actor-centered

- Source: entries/2026/06/20/wiki-Heuristic_evaluation.md
- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heuristic_evaluation

### [ACCEPT] heuristic-eval-3-to-5-evaluators-independent-first
Heuristic evaluation recommends 3–5 evaluators; evaluators must work independently before group discussion to reduce confirmation bias.
- Source: entries/2026/06/20/wiki-Heuristic_evaluation.md
- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heuristic_evaluation

### [ACCEPT] nielsen-10-usability-heuristics
Nielsen's 10 usability heuristics are: visibility of system status, match between system and real world, user control and freedom, consistency and standards, error prevention, recognition rather than recall, flexibility and efficiency of use, aesthetic and minimalist design, help users recognize/diagnose/recover from errors, and help and documentation.
- Source: entries/2026/06/20/wiki-Heuristic_evaluation.md
- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heuristic_evaluation

### [ACCEPT] shneiderman-8-golden-rules
Shneiderman's 8 Golden Rules of interface design: consistency, shortcuts for frequent users, informative feedback, dialog closure, simple error handling, easy reversal, internal locus of control, reduce short-term memory load.
- Source: entries/2026/06/20/wiki-Heuristic_evaluation.md
- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heuristic_evaluation

### [ACCEPT] heuristic-eval-qualitative-not-empirical
Heuristic evaluation produces qualitative expert observations, not empirical measurements — a key distinction from user-based testing.
- Source: entries/2026/06/20/wiki-Heuristic_evaluation.md
- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heuristic_evaluation

### [ACCEPT] hicks-law-logarithmic-decision-time
Hick's law states that decision time increases logarithmically with the number of available choices, following the formula T = b × log₂(n + 1) for equal-probability alternatives.
- Source: entries/2026/06/20/wiki-Hick27s_law.md
- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hick%27s_law

### [ACCEPT] hicks-law-plus-one-go-no-go
The +1 in Hick's law formula log₂(n+1) accounts for go/no-go uncertainty — whether to respond at all — not just which response to make.
- Source: entries/2026/06/20/wiki-Hick27s_law.md
- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hick%27s_law

### [ACCEPT] hicks-law-not-applicable-linear-search
Hick's law does not apply to linear search tasks (e.g., scanning a randomly ordered word list) — those are linear time, not logarithmic.
- Source: entries/2026/06/20/wiki-Hick27s_law.md
- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hick%27s_law

### [ACCEPT] hicks-law-named-hick-1952-hyman-1953
Hick's law is named after W. E. Hick (1952) who formalized the logarithmic model and Ray Hyman (1953) who extended it to unequal probabilities.
- Source: entries/2026/06/20/wiki-Hick27s_law.md
- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hick%27s_law

### [ACCEPT] hicks-law-unequal-probability-entropy
For choices with unequal probabilities, Hick's law uses the information-theoretic entropy H = Σ pᵢ × log₂(1/pᵢ + 1) instead of the simple log₂(n+1) form.
- Source: entries/2026/06/20/wiki-Hick27s_law.md
- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hick%27s_law

### [ACCEPT] fitts-law-motor-hicks-law-cognitive
Fitts's law governs motor execution time (reaching a target based on distance and size) while Hick's law governs the cognitive decision phase; together they model the full choice-and-act cycle in HCI.
- Source: entries/2026/06/20/wiki-Hick27s_law.md
- Source URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hick%27s_law
